### Hypothesis on corrected records: the comparison, the threshold `LIM` and the checks (lines 334 to 373)

| Line | Source | Explanation |
|---:|---|---|
| 334 | `    # ---- the hypothesis on corrected records. The csv files hold more than one interval with records: every interval` | Comment: the block on the hypothesis on corrected records starts. The csv files hold more than one interval with records. The comparison takes every interval with a record. |
| 335 | `    # (all kernels and positions) of these processes with a record, against the same kernel at the same position in` | Comment: these are intervals of all kernels and positions of these processes. Each is set against the same kernel at the same position. |
| 336 | `    # the other round of the same process (build_facts.hw_compare).` | Comment: the other interval is in the other round of the same process. The function `hw_compare` of `build_facts.py` makes the comparison. |
| 337 | `    hc = D['hw_cmp']` | `hc`: the comparison that `build_facts.py` made [Ref3]. |
| 338 | `    crow, cbor, ccl = hc['rows'], hc['border'], hc['clean']` | Takes its three lists. `crow`: every interval, of all kernels and positions, to which records can be assigned. That means: the records lie inside it, or it is the only interval that their 2 s span touches. Each entry has the field `diff`: the rate of the interval minus the rate of its other round, in GB/s. `cbor`: the records whose 2 s span touches two intervals. The sheet says that such records lie at the border of two intervals. `ccl`: the positions at which neither round has a record inside it or at its border. There `diff` is the rate of round 1 minus the rate of round 0. Only the processes with at least one record supply such positions. |
| 339 | `    sg = lambda x: '0.00' if float(g(x)) == 0 else (g(x) if x < 0 else '+' + g(x))      # a signed difference` | `sg`: a one-line function that prints a difference with 2 decimal places and its sign. A value that rounds to zero prints as `0.00`. A negative value gets its minus sign from `g`. A positive value gets a plus sign in front. |
| 340 | `    need(not any(r['other_has_records'] for r in crow)` | The build stops when an other-round interval of `crow` has a record inside it or at its border. |
| 341 | `         and not any(x['other_has_records'] for b in cbor for x in b['intervals']),` | Continues the check of line 340: the same for the other-round intervals of the border entries of `cbor`. |
| 342 | `         'no other-round interval of the comparison has a record inside it or at its border')` | The error message of the check of line 340. |
| 343 | `    rp = [r for r in crow if r['iv'] is ivp]` | `rp`: the entries of `crow` for the oldread interval `ivp`, as a list. |
| 344 | `    ri = [r for r in crow if r['iv'] is ivi]` | `ri`: the entries of `crow` for the asmread interval `ivi`, as a list. |
| 345 | `    more = [r for r in crow if not r['used']]` | `more`: the entries of the intervals that the sheet does not use. |
| 346 | `    need(len(rp) == 1 and len(ri) == 1 and len(more) + 2 == len(crow) and rp[0]['where'] == 'before or inside'` | The build stops unless each of the two used intervals has exactly one entry and all other entries are in `more`. The records of the entry of `rp` must lie `before or inside`. |
| 347 | `         and all(r['where'] == 'inside' for r in crow if r is not rp[0]),` | Continues the check of line 346: the records of every other entry must lie `inside`. |
| 348 | `         'the two used intervals with records are in the comparison; every other interval has its records inside it')` | The error message of the check of line 346. |
| 349 | `    rp, ri = rp[0], ri[0]` | From here `rp` and `ri` are the two entries themselves, not lists. |
| 350 | `    n_more, n_bor = sum(len(r['records']) for r in more), sum(len(b['records']) for b in cbor)` | `n_more`: the number of records in the entries of `more`. `n_bor`: the number of records at the borders. |
| 351 | `    need(n_more + n_bor == n_out and len(crow) > 2 and cbor and all(len(b['intervals']) == 2 for b in cbor),` | The build stops unless these two numbers add up to `n_out`, `crow` has more than 2 entries, at least one border exists, and every border lies between exactly 2 intervals. |
| 352 | `         'the records outside the used intervals lie inside other intervals or at the border of two intervals')` | The error message of the check of line 351. |
| 353 | `    LIM = 1.0       # GB/s: a difference between the two rounds above this value is counted as a difference` | `LIM` = 1.0 GB/s, the threshold of this comparison. A difference between the two rounds counts only above this value. This file sets the value. The view is not part of the fixed rules (line 377). |
| 354 | `    low = sorted((r for r in crow if r['diff'] < -LIM), key=lambda r: r['diff'])` | `low`: the entries of `crow` that lie more than 1.0 GB/s below their other round, the largest drop first. |
| 355 | `    rest = [r for r in crow if not r['diff'] < -LIM]` | `rest`: all other entries of `crow`. |
| 356 | `    cbig = [x for x in ccl if abs(x['diff']) > LIM]` | `cbig`: the positions without records at which the two rounds differ by more than 1.0 GB/s. |
| 357 | `    cmax = max(ccl, key=lambda x: abs(x['diff']))` | `cmax`: the position without records with the largest difference between the two rounds. |
| 358 | `    low_in = [r for r in low if abs(r['diff']) < abs(cmax['diff'])]` | `low_in`: the entries of `low` whose difference is smaller than the difference of `cmax`. Both are compared without their sign. |
| 359 | `    need(low and low[0] is ri and rest and all(abs(r['diff']) < LIM for r in rest) and any(r is rp for r in rest)` | First of four checks. Each stops the build unless the data show a pattern that the text below states. Here: `low` is not empty, and its largest drop is the asmread interval `ri`. `rest` is not empty, and every entry of `rest` differs by less than 1.0 GB/s. The oldread interval `rp` is in `rest`. |
| 360 | `         and rp['diff'] > 0 and min(r['diff'] for r in rest) < 0,` | Continues the check of line 359: `rp` is higher than its other round, and at least one entry of `rest` is lower than its other round. |
| 361 | `         'intervals with records: some lie more than 1 GB/s below their other round, the others differ by less than '` | The error message of the check of line 359, first part. |
| 362 | `         '1 GB/s, and oldread of L-1 is higher with records')` | The error message of the check of line 359, second part. |
| 363 | `    need(len(low) - len(low_in) == 1 and low_in and abs(ri['diff']) > abs(cmax['diff']) and cbig,` | Second check: exactly one entry of `low` differs by at least as much as `cmax`, and `ri` differs by more than `cmax`. `low_in` and `cbig` are not empty. |
| 364 | `         'only the asmread interval of p2add differs by more than the largest difference between two rounds without records')` | The error message of the check of line 363. |
| 365 | `    need((cmax['dataset'], cmax['tag'], cmax['pos']) == (hi_['dataset'], hi_['tag'], ivi['pos'] + 1) and cmax['diff'] < 0` | Third check: `cmax` lies in the process of `ivi`, at the position directly after it. Round 1 is the lower round at `cmax` (its `diff` is below 0). |
| 366 | `         and ivi['round'] == 1,` | Continues the check of line 365: `ivi` is in round 1. |
| 367 | `         'the largest difference without records is at the position directly after the asmread interval with records, '` | The error message of the check of line 365, first part. |
| 368 | `         'lower in the round of the records')` | The error message of the check of line 365, second part. |
| 369 | `    need({(x['dataset'], x['tag']) for x in ccl} == {(r['dataset'], r['tag']) for r in crow},` | Fourth check: the positions without records come from the same processes as the entries of `crow`. |
| 370 | `         'the positions without records are counted in the processes of the table')` | The error message of the check of line 369. |
| 371 | `    bdiff = [x['diff'] for b in cbor for x in b['intervals']]` | `bdiff`: the differences of the border intervals against their other round, in GB/s. |
| 372 | `    c['hw']['cmp'] = dict(n=len(crow), low=low, rest=rest, cmax=cmax, n_clean=len(ccl), n_big=len(cbig), lim=LIM)` | Stores the counts, the groups `low` and `rest`, `cmax` and `LIM` in `c['hw']['cmp']` for section 6. |
| 373 | `    c['hw']['cmp']['lst'] = lambda xs: xs[0] if len(xs) == 1 else ', '.join(xs[:-1]) + ' and ' + xs[-1]` | Stores a one-line function under the key `lst`. It joins a list of texts with commas, and with `and` before the last text. A list with one text gives that text. |

| 518 | `      'boot or the memory speed is the cause: each memory speed has one boot.'` | Continues the bullet of line 514: the data do not show that the boot or the memory speed is the cause, as each memory speed has one boot. Line 152 tests the one boot for the 8000 MT/s rows of its list. For the 7200 MT/s records that check is true whatever the run files contain. The explanation of line 152 names the tests of the 7200 MT/s boot. |
| 519 | `      % evd['T72L1']['state'].replace('started ', '').replace(' (T72A1)', ''))` | Fills in the start-state text of T72L1 from `evd`, without the word `started` and without the tag in parentheses, `(T72A1)`. |
| 520 | `    w('')` | An empty line of the sheet. It ends the list of this block. |

### Section 4.2, view: the 8000 MT/s pool L-1 to L-4 split by start state (lines 522 to 564)

| Line | Source | Explanation |
|---:|---|---|
| 522 | `    # ---- view: the 8000 MT/s pool split by the start state of the process (added after the numbers were seen)` | Comment: the view starts that splits the 8000 MT/s pool by the start state of the process. It was added after the numbers were seen. |
| 523 | `    allla = [la for d in BW12 for la in D['rx'][d]['launches'].values()]` | `allla`: every launch line of `launches.txt` in the run folders of rows L-1 to L-4. It also holds the launch lines of processes of these folders whose csv files the sheet does not use. |
| 524 | `    st = {}` | `st`: a dict for the start state of each process, empty at first. |
| 525 | `    for d in BW12:` | Starts the loop over the rows L-1 to L-4. |
| 526 | `        v = D['rx'][d]` | `v`: the entry of the row in `D['rx']`. |
| 527 | `        for tag in v['secs']:` | Starts the loop over the processes of the row. These are the processes whose csv files the sheet uses (`v['secs']`). |
| 528 | `            la = v['launches'][tag]` | `la`: the launch line of the process. |
| 529 | `            before = sorted((l for l in allla if l['rc_utc'] is not None and l['rc_utc'] <= la['utc']), key=lambda l: l['rc_utc'])` | `before`: the processes of `allla` that ended at or before this launch, by the time of their rc line, sorted by that time. A process without an rc line is left out. |
| 530 | `            prev = before[-1] if before else None` | `prev`: the process of `allla` that ended last at or before this launch. It may lie in another run folder. It is `None` when no process ended before. |
| 531 | `            st[(d, tag)] = dict(la=la, prev=prev, gap=(la['utc'] - prev['rc_utc']).total_seconds() if prev else None,` | Stores the start state of the process under its row name and tag: `la`, `prev` and `gap`. `gap` is the seconds from the rc line of `prev` to the launch line of this process. Both lines give whole seconds. `gap` is `None` when `prev` is `None`. |
| 532 | `                                first=min(v['launches'].values(), key=lambda l: l['utc']) is la, folder=v['folder'])` | Continues line 531: `first` is true when this launch is the earliest launch of its own run folder. `folder` is the run folder of the row. |
| 533 | `    idle = [k for k in st if st[k]['la']['load1'] < 1.0]` | `idle`: the processes whose launch line shows a 1-minute load average (`load1`) below 1.0. |
| 534 | `    busy = [k for k in st if st[k]['la']['load1'] >= 1.0]` | `busy`: the processes with a load of 1.0 or above. |
| 535 | `    need(idle and busy and all(st[k]['first'] for k in idle), 'the processes below load 1.0 are the first of their run folder')` | The build stops unless each group has a process and every `idle` process is the first of its run folder. |
| 536 | `    need(all(' --threads 120 ' in ' ' + st[k]['prev']['args'] + ' ' and '--cpus 0-63,64-119' in st[k]['prev']['args']` | The build stops unless two things hold for every `busy` process. First: `prev` ran with `--threads 120` and `--cpus 0-63,64-119`. The test puts a space at both ends of the arguments. So `--threads 120` must stand there as whole words. |
| 537 | `             and 0 < st[k]['gap'] <= 30 for k in busy),` | Second: the process was launched more than 0 s and at most 30 s after `prev` ended. This check reads the arguments of `prev` only. It does not read the name of a binary. All launch lines of `allla` come from the run folders of the `bwx12` rows. |
| 538 | `         'every other process of the pool started at most 30 s after a 120-thread bwx12 process')` | The error message of the check of line 536. |
| 539 | `    grp = lambda keys, k, pos: [r for r in sel(recs, dataset=set(BW12), kernel=k, pos=pos) if (r['dataset'], r['tag']) in keys]` | `grp`: a one-line function. It returns the records of rows L-1 to L-4 for one kernel and position whose process is in the given group. A process is matched by its row name and its tag. |
| 540 | `    Io, Ia, Bo, Ba = grp(idle, 'oldread', 0), grp(idle, 'asmread', 1), grp(busy, 'oldread', 0), grp(busy, 'asmread', 1)` | `Io`, `Bo`: oldread at position 0 of the groups `idle` and `busy`. `Ia`, `Ba`: asmread at position 1 of the two groups. |
| 541 | `    need(len(Io) + len(Bo) == len(P12o) and len(Ia) + len(Ba) == len(P12a), 'the two groups hold the whole pool')` | The build stops unless the two groups together have as many intervals as the pool lists `P12o` and `P12a`. So the split leaves out no interval of the pool. |
| 542 | `    gaps_ = sorted(st[k]['gap'] for k in busy)` | `gaps_`: the `gap` values of the `busy` processes in seconds, sorted. |
| 543 | `    loads = lambda keys: sorted(st[k]['la']['load1'] for k in keys)` | `loads`: a one-line function that returns the sorted loads at launch of a group. |
| 544 | `    names = lambda keys: ', '.join('%s %s' % k for k in keys)` | `names`: a one-line function that returns the processes of a group as text, each as row name and tag, joined with commas. |
| 545 | `    lsrc = lambda keys: ' '.join(dict.fromkeys(ref(os.path.join(st[k]['folder'], 'launches.txt')) for k in keys))` | `lsrc`: a one-line function that returns the `[RefN]` labels of the `launches.txt` files of a group, each file once. |
| 546 | `    w('The pool L-1 to L-4 split by the start state of the process. This view was looked at after the numbers were '` | Text of the sheet: the opening sentences of the view. The pool L-1 to L-4 is split by the start state of the process. The view was looked at after the numbers were seen. |
| 547 | `      'seen. It is not part of the fixed rules.')` | Continues the text of line 546: the view is not part of the fixed rules. |
| 548 | `    w('')` | An empty line of the sheet, before the table. |
| 549 | `    L.extend(table(['8000 MT/s group', 'Processes', 'Load at launch', 'Work before the launch', 'n', 'oldread, GB/s',` | Appends the table of the view, with one row per group. This line gives the first six of its 8 column titles: the 8000 MT/s group, the processes, the load at launch, the work before the launch, n and the oldread range in GB/s. |
| 550 | `                    'asmread, GB/s', 'Source'], [` | Column titles 7 and 8: the asmread range in GB/s and `Source`. The list of the two rows starts. |
| 551 | `        ['started at a load below 1.0', names(idle), ' and '.join(dict.fromkeys('%.2f' % x for x in loads(idle))),` | Row of the group `idle`, labelled "started at a load below 1.0": its processes (`names`) and their loads at launch with 2 decimal places. Each distinct load is printed once, joined with `and`. |
| 552 | `         'first process of its run folder', nn(rng(Io)), sp(rng(Io)), sp(rng(Ia)), 'rates %s, launch lines %s' % (src(Io), lsrc(idle))],` | Continues the row of line 551: the fixed text "first process of its run folder" (line 535 tests it), n of the oldread list `Io`, the oldread range, the asmread range, and the sources. The sources are the csv files of `Io` and the `launches.txt` files of the group. |
| 553 | `        ['started after 120-thread work', names(busy), '%.2f to %.2f' % (loads(busy)[0], loads(busy)[-1]),` | Row of the group `busy`, labelled "started after 120-thread work": its processes, and the lowest and the highest load at launch with 2 decimal places. |
| 554 | `         'a 120-thread bwx12 process had ended %.0f s to %.0f s before' % (gaps_[0], gaps_[-1]), nn(rng(Bo)), sp(rng(Bo)),` | Continues the row of line 553: the work before the launch. A 120-thread `bwx12` process had ended a shortest to a longest `gap` before (whole seconds). Then n of the oldread list `Bo` and the oldread range. |
| 555 | `         sp(rng(Ba)), 'rates %s, launch lines %s' % (src(Bo), lsrc(busy))]]))` | Continues the row of line 553: the asmread range and the sources. In both rows n is taken from the oldread list of the group. No line tests that the asmread list of the group has the same n. In the present sheet section 6 prints n of the two asmread lists, and each is equal to n of the oldread list of its group [Ref4]. |
| 556 | `    aIo, aBo, aIa, aBa = rng(Io), rng(Bo), rng(Ia), rng(Ba)` | `aIo`, `aBo`, `aIa`, `aBa`: the ranges of the four lists (lowest rate, highest rate, median, counts). |
| 557 | `    need(aBo['lo'] > aIo['hi'], '8000 MT/s: oldread after 120-thread work above oldread from a load below 1.0')` | The build stops unless the lowest oldread rate of the `busy` group is above the highest oldread rate of the `idle` group. |
| 558 | `    need(overlap(aIa, aBa), '8000 MT/s: the two asmread groups overlap')` | The build stops unless the asmread ranges of the two groups overlap. |
| 559 | `    kLB, kLaB = compare(L72o, Bo), compare(L72a, Ba)` | `kLB` compares oldread of part L (`L72o`) with `Bo`. `kLaB` compares asmread of part L (`L72a`) with `Ba`. |
| 560 | `    need(not kLB['overlap'] and kLB['dist'] < 0 and not kLaB['overlap'] and kLaB['dist'] > 0,` | The build stops unless part L lies below `Bo` for oldread and above `Ba` for asmread, both without overlap. |
| 561 | `         'part L: oldread below and asmread above the 8000 MT/s group that started after 120-thread work')` | The error message of the check of line 560. |
| 562 | `    need(overlap(rng(F72o), aIo) and overlap(rng(I72o), aIo), 'F72d12 and control I overlap the 8000 MT/s group below load 1.0')` | The build stops unless the oldread range of F72d12 overlaps the range of the `idle` group, and the oldread range of control I overlaps it too. |
| 563 | `    c['byst'] = dict(Io=aIo, Bo=aBo, Ia=aIa, Ba=aBa, idle=idle, busy=busy, gaps=gaps_, loads_busy=loads(busy),` | `c['byst']` keeps the view for later sections of the sheet. The values are stored without rounding. This line stores the four ranges, the two groups, the sorted gaps and the sorted loads of the `busy` group. |
| 564 | `                     near=-kLB['dist'], near_pct=-kLB['dist'] / aBo['lo'] * 100, asm_near=kLaB['dist'], names=names)` | Continues line 563. `near`: the lowest oldread rate of `Bo` minus the highest oldread rate of part L, in GB/s. It is `kLB['dist']` with the opposite sign. `near_pct`: `near` divided by the lowest oldread rate of `Bo`, times 100, in percent. `asm_near`: the lowest asmread rate of part L minus the highest asmread rate of `Ba`, in GB/s. The function `names` is stored too. In the present FACTS.md `near` is 4.27 GB/s. That is 0.9 % of 468.56 GB/s [Ref4]. |

This section works on `pa3`, the six cells that `facts_text.py` prepared [Ref1]. A cell is one binary with one load-only kernel in one round. From the rerun a cell holds session 1 only. Sessions 2 and 3 of part A are left out of this section. |

The 8000 MT/s range of the cell (`b80`) spans the intervals of rows A-1 and A-2 of the same binary, kernel and round. `facts_text.py` has counted how many of the 3 intervals of session 1 lie above its highest rate, below its lowest rate, or inside it [Ref1]. |
| 724 | `        if x['inside'] == 3:` | The wording of rule V3 for the cell. First case: all 3 intervals lie inside the range. |

### Section 4.3, view: the same cells at an equal pass count (lines 777 to 818)

| Line | Source | Explanation |
|---:|---|---|
| 777 | `    full = 64 * 6 + 56 * 5` | `full` is `64 * 6 + 56 * 5` = 664 passes. The code has no comment on the factors. The numbers fit 64 threads with 6 passes each plus 56 threads with 5 passes each. Rule V3 states that a thread faster than 2.684 GB/s runs a sixth pass in the 0.5 s interval [Ref5]. |
| 778 | `    w('The same intervals, restricted to an equal pass count (%d or %d passes) and kept apart by round. This view is '` | Text of the sheet: the opening of the view. The same intervals are restricted to an equal pass count and kept apart by round. The two pass counts filled in are 663 and 664 (`full - 1` and `full`). |
| 779 | `      'not part of rule V3. It was added while this sheet was built.' % (full - 1, full))` | Continues the text of line 778: this view is not part of rule V3. It was added while the sheet was built. |
| 780 | `    w('')` | An empty line of the sheet, before the table. |
| 781 | `    same = lambda rs: [r for r in rs if r['passes'] in (full - 1, full)]` | `same`: a one-line function that keeps the intervals with 663 or 664 passes. Every interval with another pass count is left out of this view. |
| 782 | `    rows, eqr = [], []` | `rows`: the rows of the table of this view. `eqr`: one entry per cell. Both are empty at first. |
| 783 | `    for x in pa3:` | Starts the loop over the six cells. |
| 784 | `        b = x['a1'] + x['a2']` | `b`: the 8000 MT/s intervals of the cell, rows A-1 and A-2 together. |
| 785 | `        s72, s80 = same(x['s1']), same(b)` | `s72`: the session 1 intervals with 663 or 664 passes. `s80`: those of rows A-1 and A-2 together. |
| 786 | `        e = dict(x=x, s72=s72, s80=s80, k=compare(s72, s80) if s72 and s80 else None)` | `e`: the entry of the cell. Its field `k` is `compare(s72, s80)` when both lists have an interval. If not, `k` is `None`. |
| 787 | `        eqr.append(e)` | Adds the entry to `eqr`. |
| 788 | `        if s80:` | Tests whether the cell has an 8000 MT/s interval with such a pass count. |

| 790 | `            cell80 = '%s (n=%d: %s)' % (one_v(s80), len(s80), per)` | `cell80`: the 8000 MT/s cell of the table: the range of `s80` or its one value (`one_v`), n, and the numbers per row. |

| 795 | `                     ('yes' if e['k']['overlap'] else 'no') if e['k'] else '-',` | The overlap cell: `yes` or `no` from `k`. A cell without a comparison shows `-`. |

### Section 4.3, view: thread rates, 120 times the per-thread average (est.) (lines 819 to 870)

| Line | Source | Explanation |
|---:|---|---|
| 819 | `    half = 120 * 0.005       # largest rounding error of 120 x (a value rounded to 0.01 GB/s)` | `half` is `120 * 0.005` = 0.6 GB/s. The csv column `per_thread_avg_GBps` is rounded to 0.01 GB/s. So its rounding error is at most 0.005 GB/s, and 120 times the column is exact to 0.6 GB/s. The comment says the same. |
| 820 | `    w('Rates of the threads in the same rows, kept apart by round. The column per_thread_avg_GBps of the csv gives '` | Text of the sheet: the opening of the view on the rates of the threads, in the same rows and kept apart by round. The sentence on the csv column `per_thread_avg_GBps` starts. |
| 821 | `      'the average rate of the 120 threads of an interval. 120 times this value is the sum of the thread rates. It '` | Continues the text of line 820: the column gives the average rate of the 120 threads of an interval. 120 times this value is the sum of the thread rates. |
| 822 | `      'is an estimate (est.): the column is rounded to 0.01 GB/s, so each sum is exact to %.1f GB/s.' % half)` | Continues the text of line 820: the sum is an estimate (est.). The column is rounded to 0.01 GB/s, so each sum is exact to a value in GB/s. That value is `half`, printed with 1 decimal place. |
| 823 | `    w('')` | An empty line of the sheet, before the table. |
| 824 | `    rows, tr = [], []` | `rows`: the rows of the table of thread rates. `tr`: one entry per cell. Both are empty at first. |
| 825 | `    av = lambda rs: [r['per_thread_avg_GBps'] for r in rs]` | `av`: a one-line function that returns the values of the field `per_thread_avg_GBps` of a record list. Line 1046 reuses the name `av` for a range. |
| 826 | `    span = lambda v, f='%.2f': (f % min(v)) if min(v) == max(v) else (f + ' to ' + f) % (min(v), max(v))` | `span`: a one-line function that prints a list of numbers as one value when all are equal. If not, it prints the lowest to the highest value. The format is `%.2f` unless the caller gives another. |
| 827 | `    for x in pa3:` | Starts the loop over the six cells. |
| 828 | `        s1, a1, a2 = av(x['s1']), av(x['a1']), av(x['a2'])` | `s1`, `a1`, `a2`: the per-thread averages of session 1, of row A-1 and of row A-2. |
| 829 | `        b = a1 + a2` | `b`: the values of `a1` and `a2` as one list. |
| 830 | `        t = dict(x=x, est=120 * (min(s1) - max(b)), agg=min(x['above']), above=min(s1) > max(b))` | `t`: the entry of the cell. `est` is 120 times the difference of the lowest value of `s1` and the highest value of `b`. It is the distance of the nearest ends by the estimate, in GB/s. `agg` is the smallest value of `x['above']`. It is the lowest `aggregate_GBps` of session 1 minus the highest one of rows A-1 and A-2 in the same round. `above` is true when every value of `s1` is above every value of `b`. |
| 831 | `        tr.append(t)` | Adds the entry to `tr`. |

A cell without a process in A-2 prints `no process`. |
| 833 | `                     span([120 * v for v in s1], '%.1f'), span([120 * v for v in b], '%.1f'), '%+.1f' % t['est'], '%+.2f' % t['agg']])` | Continues the row of line 832: 120 times the average of session 1 and of the 8000 MT/s rows, with 1 decimal place. Then `est` with the format `%+.1f` and `agg` with the format `%+.2f`. |
| 834 | `    L.extend(table(['Binary and kernel', 'Round', '7200 MT/s, session 1: average per thread, GB/s',` | Appends the table. Column titles 1 to 3: binary and kernel, round, and the average per thread of session 1 at 7200 MT/s in GB/s. |

### Section 4.6: the three visits of the `like.spec` line in the 7200 MT/s boot (lines 1042 to 1078)

| Line | Source | Explanation |
|---:|---|---|
| 1042 | `    # ---- 4.6 same boot` | Comment: section 4.6, the rows of the same boot, starts here. Lines 1042 to 1615 read no csv row and no csv column. They work on the interval records, and on the comparisons and record lists that lines 31 to 33 took from `c`. |
| 1043 | `    w('### 4.6 What the rows of the same boot show, and what follows for Q2')` | Text of the sheet: the heading of section 4.6, what the rows of the same boot show and what follows for Q2. Lines 1042 to 1615 call neither `cmps` nor `q2row`. So they add no range test to `V['q2_rows']`. The verdict texts of section 4.9 use the comparisons that were made before. A total rate is the field `GBps` of a record, in GB/s. Part A record: the csv column `aggregate_GBps`. bwx record: the sum over the threads of `bytes / elapsed_s / 1e9` [Ref2]. Section 4.7 uses two other fields of a bwx record: `mesh0_GBps` and `mesh1_GBps`, the sums per mesh (line 1221). |
| 1044 | `    w('')` | An empty line of the sheet, after the heading. |
| 1045 | `    allv = F72a + L72a + I72a` | `allv`: the asmread records of row F72d12, of part L and of control I, joined to one list. `facts_text.py` selected each list by `dataset` and `kernel`, with no position condition [Ref1]. In the file `facts.json` all of these records are at position 1. They are 2, 6 and 4 intervals. |
| 1046 | `    av = rng(allv)` | `av`: the range of this list: its lowest, its highest and its median rate. Before this line `av` was a helper (line 825). From here it is a range. |
| 1047 | `    kLI, kIF = compare(L72a, I72a), compare(I72a, F72a)` | `kLI` compares part L with control I. `kIF` compares control I with F72d12. Here `compare` works on two lists of the same 7200 MT/s boot. |
| 1048 | `    need(not kLI['overlap'] and not kIF['overlap'] and kLI['dist'] > 0 and kIF['dist'] > 0, 'three visits in rising order without overlap')` | The build stops unless part L lies above control I and control I lies above F72d12, both without overlap. |

### Section 4.9: the verdict text for the new read kernel (lines 1342 to 1383)

| Line | Source | Explanation |
|---:|---|---|
| 1342 | `    # new read kernel, part A: the two rounds are kept apart (rule V3)` | Comment: the new read kernel in part A. The two rounds are kept apart (rule V3). |
| 1343 | `    need([x['round'] for x in newread] == [1, 2] and all(len(x['above']) == 3 for x in newread),` | The build stops unless `newread` holds round 1 and round 2, in this order, and all 3 session-1 intervals of each round lie above the 8000 MT/s range of that round. |
| 1344 | `         'bw_new read of session 1: one cell per round, every interval above the 8000 MT/s range of its round')` | The error message of the check of line 1343. |
| 1345 | `    rounds = ' '.join('Round %d: %s to %s GB/s above the upper end of the round-%d range (%s GB/s).'` | `rounds`: one sentence per round. It gives the smallest and the largest value of `above` in GB/s, and the upper end of the 8000 MT/s range of the round. One value of `above` is the rate of a session-1 interval minus `b80['hi']`. `b80['hi']` is the highest rate of rows A-1 and A-2 in the same round [Ref1]. |
| 1346 | `                      % (x['round'], g(min(x['above'])), g(max(x['above'])), x['round'], g(x['b80']['hi'])) for x in newread)` | Fills in, per cell of `newread`: the round, the smallest and the largest value of `above`, the round again, and `b80['hi']`. The sentences are joined with spaces. |
| 1347 | `    n72r = {nns(x['s1']) for x in newread}` | `n72r`: n of the session-1 intervals of each round as text (`nns`), each distinct text once. |
| 1348 | `    n80r = {nn(x['b80']) for x in newread}` | `n80r`: n of the 8000 MT/s range of each round as text (`nn`), each distinct text once. |
| 1349 | `    need(len(n72r) == 1 and len(n80r) == 1, 'bw_new read: the same n in both rounds')` | The build stops unless both rounds give the same text, at 7200 MT/s and at 8000 MT/s. |

The distance `kAw['dist']` equals the distance to the whole pool (`kAao`, line 1015). That distance is above 0, so the part C group is the higher range. |

More checks: the positions of asmread in the part C group and in rows L-1 to L-4 (line 999), the order of the distances (line 1150), and no change of a wording without the interval with error records (line 918 and line 1351). |

The other 3 have 603, 663 and 664 passes [Ref4]. |

| 1439 | `      'at %s moves the write totals %s.' % (secs(sel(recs, dataset='rerun-A')), ref(os.path.join(W15, 'FINDINGS.md'))))` | Continues the note of line 1438: at the given interval length the rule moves the write totals. The file label follows. The interval length comes from `secs` over all part A records of the rerun (0.5 s in the present sheet [Ref4]). For a part A record the field `min_ms` is the constant 500 (in ms) that `build_facts.py` sets. It is not read from the csv file. An integrity check of `build_facts.py` tests that the part A script `ra.sh` starts the binary with the options `--reps 3 --min-ms 500` [Ref3]. |
| 1440 | `    w('- A cell shows n: lowest / median / highest rate in GB/s. The copy kernels count read plus written bytes.')` | The second note: a cell shows n, then the lowest, the median and the highest rate in GB/s. The copy kernels count read plus written bytes. This file does not compute that count. `build_facts.py` stops unless the header line that the binary prints for each copy and asmcopy process of part A and of rows A-1 to A-3 holds the words `copy counts read+write bytes` [Ref3]. |
| 1441 | `    w('')` | An empty line of the sheet, before the table. |
| 1442 | `    rows = []` | `rows`: the rows of the table, empty at first. |
| 1443 | `    for k in ('write', 'copy', 'asmwrite', 'asmcopy'):` | Starts the loop over the four kernels write, copy, asmwrite and asmcopy. |
| 1444 | `        for b in ('bw_old', 'bw_new'):` | Starts the loop over the two binaries `bw_old` and `bw_new`. Each pair of kernel and binary gives one table row. |
| 1445 | `            row = [k, b]` | `row`: the table row. It starts with the kernel and the binary. |
| 1446 | `            for cond in (dict(dataset='A-1'), dict(dataset='rerun-A', session=1), dict(dataset='rerun-A', session=2),` | Starts the loop over the four data columns. This line names three of them: row A-1, and sessions 1 and 2 of part A of the rerun. |
| 1447 | `                         dict(dataset='rerun-A', session=3)):` | Continues the list of line 1446: session 3 of part A of the rerun. |
| 1448 | `                rs = sel(recs, binary=b, kernel=k, **cond)` | `rs`: every record of that binary and kernel in the row or session. Both rounds go into one cell. |
| 1449 | `                a = rng(rs)` | `a`: the range of these records. |
| 1450 | `                row.append('%d: %s / %s / %s' % (a['n'], g(a['lo']), g(a['med']), g(a['hi'])))` | Adds the cell: n, then the lowest, the median and the highest `GBps`, with 2 decimal places. In the file `facts.json` every cell has n = 6: 3 intervals of each of the 2 rounds. |
| 1451 | `            rows.append(row)` | Adds the row to the table. |
| 1452 | `    L.extend(table(['Kernel', 'Binary', '2026-11-13 (A-1), 8000 MT/s', 'Session 1, 7200 MT/s', 'Session 2, 7200 MT/s',` | Appends the table. Column titles 1 to 5: `Kernel`, `Binary`, row A-1 of 2026-11-13 at 8000 MT/s, and sessions 1 and 2 at 7200 MT/s. |
| 1453 | `                    'Session 3, 7200 MT/s'], rows))` | Column title 6: session 3 at 7200 MT/s. Then the rows follow. |

### Section 6: headings, the check of the integrity checks, and a count for part A (lines 1455 to 1468)

| Line | Source | Explanation |
|---:|---|---|
| 1455 | `    # =============================================================================== 6` | Comment: a separator line with the section number 6. |
| 1456 | `    w('## 6. What is established, what is not, and hypotheses')` | Text of the sheet: the heading of section 6, what is established, what is not, and hypotheses. |
| 1457 | `    w('')` | An empty line of the sheet, after the heading. |
| 1458 | `    w('### Established')` | Text of the sheet: the heading of the first list, "Established". |
| 1459 | `    w('')` | An empty line of the sheet, after the heading. |
| 1460 | `    nck = len(D['checks'])` | `nck`: the number of integrity checks in `D['checks']`. |
| 1461 | `    need(all(k['ok'] for k in D['checks']), 'all integrity checks pass')` | The build stops unless every integrity check of `build_facts.py` has a true `ok` field. `build_facts.py` runs these checks before the text is built, and it only stores their results [Ref3]. So this line is the place where a failed check stops the build. From 2026-11-24 on, a new build in folder S stops here. `go.sh` was edited on that day and no longer has the sha256 that the list `local.sha` holds for it. So the check `sha-local` fails. |
| 1462 | `    r2 = 0` | `r2`: a counter, 0 at first. It counts the load-only cells of part A in which round 2 lies above round 1. |
| 1463 | `    cells = 0` | `cells`: a counter of all these cells, 0 at first. |
| 1464 | `    for s_ in (1, 2, 3):` | Starts the loop over the sessions 1, 2 and 3. |
| 1465 | `        for b, k in (('bw_new', 'read'), ('bw_old', 'asmread'), ('bw_new', 'asmread')):` | Starts the loop over the three load-only kernels of part A: `bw_new read`, `bw_old asmread` and `bw_new asmread`. A cell is one session with one of them. That gives 9 cells. |
| 1466 | `            a1_, a2_ = (sel(recs, dataset='rerun-A', session=s_, round=rd, binary=b, kernel=k) for rd in (1, 2))` | `a1_`: the records of the cell in round 1. `a2_`: the records in round 2. In the present sheet the table of section 3.1 shows each such list as one process with 3 intervals [Ref4]. |
| 1467 | `            cells += 1` | Counts the cell in `cells`. |
| 1468 | `            r2 += rng(a2_)['lo'] > rng(a1_)['hi']` | `r2` increases by 1 when the lowest rate of round 2 is above the highest rate of round 1. Then the two ranges do not overlap. In the present sheet `r2` is 8 of 9 cells [Ref4]. |

Then the `low` differences as positive numbers, smallest first, joined by the stored function `lst`. `diff` is the rate of such an interval minus the rate of the same kernel at the same position in the other round of the same process. |

## References

- [Ref1] `/home/jhan/workspace/titan/cache-perf/crb/tmp/agent-scratch/w120-7200-2026-11-17/facts_text.py`: Defines `need`, `_q`, `g`, `sp`, `pc`, `pcr`, `nn`, `nns`, `table` and the fixed texts (lines 18 to 137), `med` and `analyse` with `pool` (lines 279 to 330) and `compare` (lines 333 to 336). Its function `render` defines `q2row` (lines 359 to 368), prepares the record lists, the comparisons and `pa3` (lines 413 to 439), calls `section4` (lines 1458 to 1464) and then builds section 0 from `c` (line 1467).
- [Ref2] `/home/jhan/workspace/titan/cache-perf/crb/tmp/agent-scratch/w120-7200-2026-11-17/facts_data.py`: Defines `sel`, `rng`, `overlap`, `gap` and `width_pct` (lines 442 to 485). Its parsers compute the rate of an interval: `read_k120` takes the column aggregate_GBps of a part A csv file (lines 264 to 304), and `bwx_intervals` sums bytes / elapsed_s / 1e9 over the T rows of a bwx csv file (lines 356 to 383). Lines 9 to 13 and 436 to 437 state the two kinds of rate.
- [Ref3] `/home/jhan/workspace/titan/cache-perf/crb/tmp/agent-scratch/w120-7200-2026-11-17/build_facts.py`: Builds the interval records with `add_k120` and `add_bwx` (lines 119 to 160). Sets the fields `speed_MTps`, `modules` and `boot` of a record: from try6.txt for the 7200 MT/s rows (line 56), and from the DIMM report for the 8000 MT/s rows (function `state_of`, lines 58 to 64). Its integrity check `boot` compares the boot ids of the state lines and of the probe.txt files with try6.txt (lines 674 to 681). Names the rows of the earlier runs and the positions, kernels and tags that become records (lines 30 to 31 and 182 to 284). Places the hardware error records with `hw_records` and `hw_compare` (lines 293 to 397). Writes `V` and the interval records to facts.json and rounds their decimal numbers to 3 places (lines 1016 to 1029). The function `run_checks` (lines 653 to 894) holds the integrity checks. `main` is in lines 1033 to 1052. It calls `run_checks` before `render`.
- [Ref4] `/home/jhan/workspace/titan/cache-perf/crb/tmp/agent-scratch/w120-7200-2026-11-17/FACTS.md`: The output file. Sections 4, 5 and 6 are lines 436 to 949. The word list is lines 38 to 89. Lines 95 and 96 define Q1 and Q2. Section 2 (lines 275 to 302) lists the decisions (a) to (r). Section 1.4 (lines 211 to 239) lists the integrity checks.
- [Ref5] `/home/jhan/workspace/titan/cache-perf/crb/tmp/agent-scratch/w120-7200-2026-11-17/baselines-and-rules.txt`: The text of the rules V1 to V4, F1 and F2 and the fixed list of 8000 MT/s baselines, written before go.sh started (183 lines).
- [Ref6] `/home/jhan/workspace/titan/cache-perf/crb/tmp/agent-scratch/kernels-2026-11-13/bandwidth.c`: Source of bw_new. It computes aggregate_GBps as total bytes / longest per-thread time / 1e9 and prints it and the three per-thread columns with 2 decimal places (lines 584 to 603). After the intervals of a kernel it prints one `best` line with the highest aggregate_GBps (lines 599 to 605). A `thread` line of the option --per-thread also has 2 decimal places (line 612). bandwidth.orig.c, the source of bw_old, has the same computation and format in its lines 427 to 448. Limit: the records of 2026-11-13 show two texts of bandwidth.c. This local file has the sha256 f1985072.... The file probe.txt of the timed run of 18:12:22 UTC (folder kernels-20261113T181222Z, row A-1) recorded the sha256 c1406e02... for bandwidth.c. The probe.txt files of the three result folders `kernels-20261113T181210Z`, `kernels-20261113T181222Z` and `kernels-20261113T183218Z` give the same sha256 for the binary bw_new (fd7c14df...). So the machine code is not in doubt. The two texts differ in comment lines only. An edit of 2026-11-13 18:32:07 UTC replaced 2 comment lines above `k_read` with the 5 comment lines 178 to 182 of the local text. The record of the Claude Code session of that day holds the edit (`/home/jhan/.claude/projects/-home-jhan-workspace-titan-cache-perf-crb/7663f0d2-7d09-4571-b80c-92526358fa86.jsonl`, line 1000). With this edit reversed, the local text has the sha256 value of the earlier text (c1406e02...). No code line differs.
